I mean, even back in elementary school, it was always a problem. My teachers were constantly moving my seat because I talked out of turn a lot. And I was always forgetting my homework, too. When we had worksheets, I'd just rush right through them and end up making all these careless mistakes. Like, I’ll be trying to work on a job application, and then a car drives by outside, or my phone lights up, or just some random idea pops into my head. And before I even notice it, I'm doing something completely different and the application is just sitting there. It's really frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're trying to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Whenever I'm sitting with someone, my leg is usually bouncing. Or I'll find myself clicking a pen or just messing with my keys. Most of the time, I don't even realize I'm doing it until I catch myself. But yeah, just sitting completely still is pretty tough for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble staying focused and sitting still, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. I mean, even back in elementary school, it was always a problem. My teachers were constantly moving my seat because I talked out of turn a lot. And I was always forgetting my homework, too. When we had worksheets, I'd just rush right through them and end up making all these careless mistakes. So, yeah, it's definitely been going on since I was a kid.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that one. I mean, I don't think I do things without thinking, at least not any more than anyone else does. Like I said just a minute ago, I don't feel like I just jump into things blindly. I mean, maybe I've done it once or twice like anyone else, but no specific examples are coming to mind right now. I just haven't really noticed it being a thing for me.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with focus and restlessness caused problems for you at work, school, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely has. Especially when it comes to work and trying to get a career going. I've been trying to become an automotive technician, and I know I can do the hands-on part of the job just fine. But I keep missing these training deadlines, or I'll mess up the applications and have to redo them. It's just... I can't seem to get organized enough to actually move forward with it. So yeah, it's definitely causing some problems.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with focus and restlessness caused problems for you in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, not really, no. I mean, I haven't noticed it causing any major issues with my friends or family. We get along fine. It's really just the work and career stuff that's taking the hit, like I was saying. It's just when I have to actually sit down and handle the applications or the deadlines for the auto tech training that everything falls apart. Quiet stuff is pretty hard for me. Like, if my family is trying to watch a movie or if I try to just sit down and read a book... I can't really just sit there. I end up starting to talk, or I'm getting up and walking around, or just making noise. I don't mean to bother anyone, it's just really hard to stay still and quiet for that long.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do that a lot. I cut people off all the time, especially my mom or my counselor. It's like... an answer just pops into my head while they're talking, and I just say it before they're even finished. I don't really mean to be rude, it just kind of comes out.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blur out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's pretty much what I just meant. I end up starting to talk, or I'm getting up and walking around, or just making noise. I don't think it happens in most situations, or at least I haven't really caught myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your symptoms of inattention, hyperactivity, and impulsivity as causing significant distress or impairment in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it definitely holds me back. Like... I'm trying to become an automotive technician. I know for a fact I can handle the hands-on work, that part is fine. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, actually. Even back in elementary school, I had a lot of trouble with that kind of stuff. My teachers were always having to move my seat because I'd talk out of turn all the time.
